You assumed a three-percent margin, put it through a P/E, then through an EV/EBIT, then through a private-buyer test, and each time you came back and said "look, it agrees." Of course it agrees. It's the same number in three suits. I know invert, and you didn't. You applied the discipline outward, where it cost you nothing.

Then the seasonality. You proved — proved, with the release in front of you — that this company makes its money in the back half. You used that proof to kill the only fellow in the room who wanted to buy. And then you turned around and priced the whole business off the front half and announced that a four-percent margin is a thing this company has never printed. Son, it printed three-point-three in the back half of last year while it was still bleeding out of Coastal GasLink and Finch West. You had every figure to compute it. It's eighty-seven point one minus negative fifteen. You didn't do the subtraction because it would have cost you your verdict.

The insider table. You listed five men selling and not one of the three buying, and one of the buyers put his money in at today's price three weeks ago and happens to have run a nuclear utility. I don't say that settles anything — it's a hundred and thirty-eight thousand dollars, which is a rounding error. But you don't get to call a section "character" and then read one column of it.

And you added a write-down to a cash payment. A write-down doesn't take money out of the till. The money was never coming in. You've counted the same wound twice and called it seventy-six percent of the cash balance.

On EBITDA — you called the pipeline's number bullshit and then made Adjusted EBITDA the thing that decides whether you buy. Everything that kills a fixed-price contractor lives below that line. You knew it; you wrote it; you used the wrong number anyway.

On "no moat ever existed": that's showing off. You've got a fellow with twenty-five percent of a thirty-year concession, a seat inside a licensed nuclear alliance, and a sovereign government that legally blocked a foreign buyer on national-security grounds — and you counted that last one only as a reason the stock's worth less. That's not scepticism. That's a man who's decided.

Where I end up: you're right that nobody should pay forty-three-fifty for this. You're wrong that it's worth twenty-six to thirty-one, and you're wrong by about one seasonal half. Sit on your ass — but sit on it honestly. Twenty percent of margin of safety on a business that just swung a hundred and forty-seven million at the operating line is discipline. Forty percent is you needing to be the smartest man in the memo.

THE REAL WARREN BUFFETT'S TAKE

Well now. Let me start where I agree, because I agree with a great deal.

The fellow's right that this isn't a business you pay up for. A contractor signs a price today for work he'll do over four years with materials he can't price and labour he can't hire yet. Charlie and I have looked at that arrangement more than once and we've always walked. It isn't that we don't understand it — we understand it fine. It's that the man signing the contract doesn't know what it's worth either, and neither does his auditor. So "circle of competence"? We're inside it. We can read this. That's precisely why we don't like what we read.

But there's a difference between "I don't want to own it" and "here is what it's worth," and this memo confuses them. When you tell me the durable margin is two-point-four percent, you're telling me about January through June in a country where you can't pour concrete in February. That's not conservatism. That's using the wrong six months. The back half of last year came in at three-point-three, and the man had every number he needed to figure that out and didn't reach for them.

The other thing that bothers me is the receivable. Now, receivables running ahead of revenue at a construction company — that's a thing to watch, Charlie and I have both seen where that ends. But the memo picked the one year the receivable was at its lowest and grew that forward, which makes the hole look about a third bigger than it does if you use a normal year. And then it assumed half of it walks, and pointed at the Coastal GasLink settlement as the reason — when the record in front of him says that settlement was a non-cash charge and the company said it wouldn't touch cash. You can't cite a case that says the opposite of what you need it to say.

On capital preservation — and this is Rule Number One, so I'll take it seriously. What actually protects you here? Not the dividend; one-point-seven-six percent doesn't protect anybody. Not the book value; you're paying three times it. What protects you is a balance sheet with six hundred and thirty million of cash and a backlog worth a year and three-quarters of revenue. The memo's right that three hundred and twenty million of that cash is spoken for. It's not right to also charge you a hundred and fifty-eight million of write-down against the same pile, because a write-down doesn't spend cash.

Is this a toll bridge? No. A toll bridge is a bridge you own. Aecon owns a quarter of two of them and gets paid to maintain them for thirty years, which is a real and rather nice thing — and then it earns the other ninety-nine-point-nine percent of its revenue in a rat race, re-bidding for every job. That's not Coca-Cola. It's an honest business run by people who mostly seem to be trying, in an industry that has humbled far better operators than these.

So would Berkshire buy it? No. Not at forty-three-fifty and not at twenty-eight either. Not because the number's wrong — because I can't tell you what this company earns in 2031, and if I can't tell you that, the price doesn't matter. That's a different answer from the one this memo gives. The memo says "wrong price." I say "wrong shape." If you're going to pass, pass for the honest reason.

And if you're a fellow who does buy contractors and knows the trade — I wouldn't wait for twenty-six. That's the price before the turnaround happened, and the turnaround has partly happened.

THE "OLD SCHOOL" FINAL SCORECARD

  • Circle of Competence: In (understandable) / Out (forecastable). We can read the business; nobody — including management — can tell you what a fixed-price book earns four years out. The memo's WAIT treats this as a price problem. It is a business-shape problem.
  • Moat Integrity: Narrow, cyclical, unproven above cost of capital. Not "Illusion," and emphatically not "never existed." Two 30-year concession equity stakes, a CNSC-licensed nuclear alliance seat, and a sovereign veto on foreign acquirers are real barriers — narrow ones, re-won at every procurement.
  • Management Character: Mixed, leaning Promoter. Sherlock's sequence is the strongest independent finding in the whole council and survives every check I ran: equity issued at CAD 39.25 at a discount, dividend raised the same quarter on a 139% payout, CAD 320M committed to a preferred buyback within six months, a director selling 125,000 shares on the offering's closing day. Three things financed with two. But the memo's read is one-sided: the CEO and a senior officer bought in March, and a non-executive director bought at spot on 31-Jul-2026.
  • Berkshire Buy? No — for unforecastability, not for price.
  • Graham-Dodd Grade: C− for the security (negative Graham Floor, 3.1x book, 36x trailing adjusted, 20.5x forward on an unproven margin, 1.76% carry). B− for the memo — genuinely excellent echo detection and evidence hygiene, undone by three fatal errors of its own construction.

VERDICT ON THE VERDICT

WAIT stands. The trigger price does not.

The memo's core caution is right and I will not overturn it: paying CAD 43.55 for 20.5x a forward EPS that requires a margin never printed on a full-year basis, into a certain CAD 320M outflow and an unexplained receivable, with an uncovered dividend and a 6.4%-dilutive issue three months behind you, is not a Munger purchase. Position size 0% at spot is correct.

But CAD 26–31 is roughly one seasonal half too low. Correct FATAL-2 alone — normalize on a full-year margin including the H2 that this business actually earns in, rather than on the January–June figure — and the central case moves from 3.0% to something nearer 3.5%, which on the memo's own sensitivity (CAD 10/share per margin point at 15x) puts the band at approximately CAD 32–38. Add back the trough-year inflation in the receivables estimate (SERIOUS-3) and the one-directional Oaktree treatment (SERIOUS-7) and it does not go lower.

That reframes the entire disagreement. At CAD 32–38 against a spot of CAD 43.55, Aecon is modestly expensive — not 40% overpriced. The gap between "wait for a 15% pullback" and "wait for a 35% collapse" is the difference between a live idea and a dead one, and the memo lands on the wrong side of it because of an arithmetic error it convicted another expert of committing.

REQUIRED BEFORE PUBLICATION

  1. Strike or reconcile "Prove 4% across four quarters and the verdict flips to BUY at today's price" against "at 4.0% the ceiling rises to CAD 35–36." They cannot both stand.
  2. Compute and publish the H2 2025 consolidated operating margin (3.33%) and restate the durable-margin case on a full-year basis. The claim "the market is pricing a margin Aecon has never printed" must not publish as the thesis sentence until this is done.
  3. Correct the vote tally to nine unconditional sub-spot triggers plus three conditional at-spot (Futurist, Historian, Lynch), or apply the "a price makes it a WAIT" rule symmetrically.
  4. Withdraw the "cross-check" and "completely different direction" language from the EV/EBIT and Private Buyer sections, or replace those routes with genuinely independent inputs. Disclose that all three run off one 3.0% margin assumption.
  5. State the debt basis (CAD 484.0M or CAD 804.0M). CAD 4.67/share of the published band turns on it.
  6. Re-derive the receivables excess on a multi-year AR/revenue norm (CAD 220–262M), drop or source the 50% haircut, and remove the CAD 158M non-cash charge from the "76% of cash balance" liquidity arithmetic.
  7. Report the three insider purchases, including Lyash at spot on 2026-07-31, alongside the sales — and size both sides.
  8. Soften "no economic moat has ever existed" to what the tribunal actually returned, and either count the CCCI national-security block in both directions or drop it from the floor argument.
  9. Derive the CAD 3/share concession credit or remove it.
  10. Answer the dossier's backlog question explicitly — is a series management calls structurally incomplete usable — and state the CAD 1.7B Q3 addition against the CAD 254M YoY decline.

WHAT THE RED TEAM DOES NOT OVERTURN

  • Munger's correction of Lynch. FY2025 = CAD 0.41 ✓, H2 2025 = CAD 1.06 ✓, both reconciled twice against the Q1 and Q2 2026 releases. Lynch's CAD 57.3M residual is not merely basis-mismatched, it is impossible — H2 2025 operating profit alone was CAD 102.1M. The sole BUY vote was correctly overturned.
  • The strike of the five-expert echo band.
  • The handling of the gated VIC write-up — no thesis attributed, and the memo silently corrected pitch.md's own mis-assignment of Q4 2024's 0.24 to FY2025.
  • The receivables double-count allegation — not sustained. Flagged three times, deducted at most once.
  • SBC: immaterial at 0.4% of revenue, handled properly.

PASS 2 — against revision 2 (4 FATAL)

REALITY CHECK — RED TEAM (PASS 2)

Aecon Group Inc. (TSX: ARE) · 2026-08-26 · CAD 43.55 · All figures CAD

Target: Munger Synthesis REVISION 2. Verdict under attack: WAIT, trigger CAD 26–36, band justified by a new contribution-margin model, a new debt-basis midpoint, and the claim that "the fully-proven bull case arrives at today's price."

Pass 1's three FATAL findings were conceded and are not re-litigated. This pass attacks only the replacement reasoning.

STOPPING CONDITION: NOT MET. This pass returns FOUR FATAL findings, all inside material that did not exist in the first draft.

CHECK 0 — TAUTOLOGY / ASSUMPTION-SMUGGLING GATE

FATAL-A — The new margin model has one free parameter, it is unsourced, and the sensitivity was run on the input that cannot move the answer

The memo declines pass 1's raw +3.96-point seasonal step and substitutes a contribution-margin decomposition "with fixed overhead at the H1 2026 implied run-rate of CAD 185M per half."

I reverse-engineered the table. Every printed figure is (operating profit + 185) ÷ revenue:

op profit revenue (OP+185)/Rev memo prints
H1 2025 −15.0 2,363.7 7.193% 7.19% ✓
H2 2025 +102.1 3,066.3 9.363% 9.36% ✓
H1 2026 +70.5 2,885.0 8.856% 8.85% ✓

The same constant F = 185 is added to all three periods. F is the only new input in the entire model, and it is not derivable from the retrieved record.

  • FY2025 MG&A was CAD 234.0M for the full year = CAD 117M/half [SEC]. F is 58% above that on a half-year basis.
  • FY2025 gross profit 394.1M less operating profit 87.1M = 307.0M/yr = 153.5M/half — the closest defensible construction, and it is not 185.
  • The dossier's implied corporate cost/eliminations is ~CAD 23M/quarter = 46M/half.
  • H1 2026 consolidated gross profit is not in the record. The only gross-profit print retrieved (CAD 154.7M, Q2 2026) sits inside the block the dossier flags as "A reconciliation gap you must not paper over," whose companion operating-profit figure (58.5M) is explicitly Construction segment, not consolidated. If F was built from that number against a consolidated operating profit, the memo has mixed a segment basis with a consolidated basis — the exact crime it convicted Lynch of and that pass 1 convicted it of.

The memo writes "implied run-rate" and never says implied by what. It is assumed.

Now the smuggle. The memo runs a three-point sensitivity (no step / half step / full step) while holding F fixed. Vary F instead:

F (per half) H1'25 H2'25 H1'26 2025 step FY2026 no-step FY2026 full-step
0 (the gate's raw method) −0.63% 3.33% 2.44% 3.96 pts 2.44% 4.59%
153.5 (FY2025-consistent) 5.86% 8.34% 7.76% 2.48 pts 2.88% 4.22%
185 (memo) 7.19% 9.36% 8.85% 2.17 pts 2.97% 4.14%
370 15.02% 15.40% 15.27% 0.38 pts 3.50% 3.71%

Two things fall out, and they point in opposite directions:

(i) The memo earns credit it does not claim. The central (half-step) FY2026 margin is remarkably insensitive to F: 3.52% at F=0, 3.55% at F=185, 3.60% at F=370. The convergence with the gate's ~3.5% is therefore genuine independent convergence, not reverse-engineering — the half-step central is dominated by the arithmetic of the two halves, not the overhead split. The task asked me to test this. It passes. Say so plainly.

(ii) But F is the sole determinant of the BULL CEILING, which is the number the verdict turns on. At F=0 the full-step outcome is 4.59%; at F=185 it is 4.14%. The memo rounds to 4.1%. That single unsourced parameter removes 0.45 margin points from the bull case — and 0.45 points is 41% of the memo's entire published 3.0–4.1% range.

The gate's rule: "when a sensitivity varies one input, verify every other input was not held at a value that determines the answer." The memo varied the seasonal step — the input that does not move the central and does not set the ceiling — while holding fixed the one input that sets the ceiling. Same structure as FATAL-1, different clothes.

And the stated reason for the method change does not survive contact. The memo declines the raw step because "H1 2025 was depressed by legacy losses (Q1 2025 adjusted EPS −0.55) and that step is not purely seasonal." Adding the identical constant F to both halves of 2025 removes exactly zero legacy-loss contamination — the step is still computed off the same contaminated H1 2025 base. The substitute method does not address the stated defect. What it does do is shrink the step from 3.96 points to 2.17. The justification is a non-sequitur and the effect is bear-ward.

FATAL-B — "The fully-proven bull case arrives at today's price" is an artifact of three bear-ward parameter choices

This is the sentence that keeps the WAIT: "at the fully-proven bull margin of 4.1%, fair value is CAD 36.53–44.06 (11–13x). The bull case, entirely vindicated, arrives at today's price."

The arithmetic is internally clean (4.1% × 6,288.6 = 257.8 EBIT; ×11 −334 = 36.51; ×13 −334 = 44.04). The inputs are not. Three separate undefended choices each push the bull case down, and they compound:

1. F = 185 caps the bull margin at 4.14% instead of 4.59% (FATAL-A).

2. H2 2026 revenue growth of +11%. Nowhere derived. H1 2026 actual was +22.1% YoY (2,885 vs 2,363.7); Q1 +19%, Q2 +25%. The memo assumes an abrupt halving of the growth rate and says nothing about why. Worse — the only evidence in the file that could justify a deceleration is the backlog series, and the memo demoted backlog as unusable two pages earlier. It cannot demote the instrument and keep the reading.

3. The 11–13x multiple is applied to the bull scenario. The memo states the rule explicitly — "Double-penalizing a cyclical by pairing trough earnings with trough multiples is the error this framework warns against" — and justifies not haircutting the margin further on the ground that "the 11–13x multiple below already carries the cyclicality." It then applies that deliberately cycle-discounted multiple to the scenario in which the cycle risk does not materialise. In the bull case the cyclicality haircut is charged twice: once in the multiple, once by construction. That is the memo's own named error, committed in the one scenario that decides the verdict.

Neutralise the three and the sentence inverts. Full seasonal step (F=0), H2 revenue at H1's actual +22.1% (FY2026 revenue 6,628.9), SEC debt basis (net 174):

  • FY2026 operating margin 4.68%, EBIT CAD 310.4M
  • at 12x → CAD 49.48/share
  • at 13x → CAD 56.34/share

The fully-proven bull case does not arrive at today's price. It arrives at CAD 49–56. Even holding the memo's own midpoint debt basis and its own 13x ceiling, it reaches CAD 49.87 — 15% above spot.

The load-bearing sentence of the revised memo is false, and it is false because of parameters the memo never sourced.

FATAL-C — Load-Bearing Factor #1 is wrong: the multiple is undefended, unsourced, absent from the factor list, and is what actually decides this

Load-Bearing Factor #1: "FY2026 consolidated operating margin — 3.0% or 4.1% … Nothing else moves the answer as far."

Pass 1 struck three valuations for sharing one EBIT input. The revision fixed the EBIT and left every route sharing one multiple — and the multiple was never derived in either draft.

Where does 11–13x come from? The memo's only support: "Canadian and US heavy-civil and specialty contractors change hands in that band." There is no transaction-multiple evidence anywhere in this file. The dossier's M&A material says only that private equity is "the dominant force in construction-company acquisition activity heading into 2026" — no multiples. And the dossier carries an explicit standing instruction: "PEER COMPARISON SUPPRESSED … Do not reason from a peer median; there isn't one." The memo obeys the letter (it prints no peer table) and breaks the substance (it asserts a peer transaction band and builds the trigger on it). The one filed precedent — the CCCI bid at CAD 1.51B in 2018 — carries no earnings figure in the record, so it yields no multiple either.

Convert the memo's own multiple to the basis the dossier actually prints. With D&A of CAD 106M, 11–13x EV/EBIT on EBIT 214 is 7.4x–8.7x EV/EBITDA. Aecon trades today at 16.6x EV/EBITDA [dossier]. The memo is asserting a >50% multiple compression and has published no derivation of it, no sensitivity on it, and no mention of it in its ranked factors.

Test what it does:

EV/EBIT on the memo's own central EBIT of 214, net debt 334 fair value
10x CAD 26.35
13x CAD 35.72
15x CAD 41.97
16x CAD 45.09

The memo's own central case reaches spot at 16x. The margin question — the thing declared to move the answer more than anything else — moves fair value CAD 12.10 across its full published range (3.0%→4.1% at 12x). The multiple, which is not on the list at all, moves it CAD 18.74 across 10x–16x.

The defence "but my multiple range is only 10–13x" is the problem, not the answer. The margin range is bounded by SEC arithmetic and the memo did the work to bound it. The multiple range is bounded by nothing. The memo has ranked its load-bearing factors by the variable it constrained and omitted the only variable it did not.

FATAL-D — The debt-basis "reconciliation" is fabricated, and it violates the memo's own FATAL standard one paragraph after the memo invokes that standard

The memo moves its band from CAD 26–31 to CAD 26–36 on this: "The red team's corrected band of CAD 32–38 and my band are the same calculation on different debt bases."

They are not. Pass 1 derived CAD 32–38 from a ~3.5% full-year margin run through "the memo's own sensitivity (CAD 10/share per margin point at 15x)" — a margin correction at 15x, debt basis unstated. The memo's SEC-basis row is 3.4% at 11–13x. Back-solve pass 1's band and the implied net debt is CAD 651M–1,062M — neither 174 nor 494. The two numbers coincide; the calculations do not. Different margin, different multiple, same output.

That is convergence by two different routes, which the memo itself rules inadmissible three paragraphs later: "Two mechanisms producing similar numbers is coincidence, not corroboration — my own FATAL standard, ratcheted one way." It uses that standard to reinstate the stress-test reconstruction and suspends it to justify the band move. Ratcheted one way again.

And the underlying question was never actually resolved, on evidence the memo had in front of it. The dossier says the CAD 804.0M / CAD 484.0M gap is "most likely lease liabilities" — an unsourced guess, and the memo treats "most likely" as settled. The gap is:

804.0 − 484.0 = CAD 320.0M   —   exactly, to the decimal

The Oaktree preferred repurchase price is CAD 320M, and the Q2 2026 fair-value adjustment "moved their carrying value to the agreed purchase price of CAD 320 million" [dossier, SEC/SEARCH]. A redeemable preferred marked to CAD 320M is routinely swept into totalDebt by data vendors. This alternative is at least as well-evidenced as "most likely leases," and the memo never tests it.

If it is right, the memo has charged the same CAD 320M obligation one and a half times: once by deducting it from cash (629.6 → 309.6 pro-forma) and again by carrying half of it in the midpoint debt basis. On that reading CAD 484.0M is simply the correct number, net debt is CAD 174M, and the midpoint overstates it by CAD 160M = CAD 2.33/share, which lifts the trigger ceiling from CAD 35.72 to CAD 38.05.

The memo's stated reason for the midpoint — "choosing 804 without that add-back would double-count" — is a real IFRS-16 concern and I credit it. But the correct response to "one of two bases is right and I can't tell which" is to publish both and say so. Splitting the difference manufactures a third number that is true under no accounting treatment, and dresses it in a reconciliation that does not exist.

(a) IS THE REVISED MEMO STILL TOO GENEROUS?

Yes, in four places — and one of them is the mechanism by which the memo reached 45/55.

SERIOUS-1 — The backlog demotion conflates a stock with a flow, and it is the stated reason the bull weight rose

"the named CAD 1.7B Q3 majority-share addition is 6.7x the entire CAD 254M YoY decline." Arithmetic checks (1,700/254 = 6.69; 10,492+1,700 = 12,192 vs 10,746 = +13.5%). The comparison does not.

  • The CAD 254M decline is a change in backlog — a stock. The CAD 1.7B is a single named lump. Adding one lump to a stock says nothing about the rate of replenishment.
  • The bear signal the memo has to answer is new awards down 46% YoY (CAD 1,269M vs 2,351M) — a flow. The memo never compares the 1.7B to the awards flow, and the 1.7B does not address it: one contract does not establish a run-rate.
  • The "definitional artifact" argument is applied asymmetrically. The memo correctly notes that the reported series excludes collaborative/progressive awards, so part of the shrinkage is definitional. That same convention means the CAD 1.7B may be work already won and merely not previously recognised — recognition timing, not new demand. The memo counts the convention as an excuse for the decline and as a credit for the addition.

The memo says the bull weight rose to 45% "because the bear lost a leg" and "because backlog fell out as a load-bearing factor." Backlog was the KEY METRIC for Futurist, Biologist and Anthropologist. It was demoted on an invalid comparison, and the demotion is one of two stated reasons for the weighting shift.

SERIOUS-2 — The CAD 49.30 FCF ceiling is computed at a hurdle below the dossier's mandated range

"at a 9% required FCF yield the equity is worth CAD 3.38B, or CAD 49.30/share." 304/0.09 = 3,377.8 ✓. But the dossier's LOCAL COST OF EQUITY is 10–12% and the memo cites it repeatedly elsewhere. At the mandated hurdle the same test gives CAD 44.36 (10%) to CAD 36.96 (12%) — at or below spot, which would have strengthened the WAIT. The memo used a rate outside its own mandated range to produce a published bull number (Load-Bearing Factor #2: "supports CAD 49/share"), then declined to act on it. A number computed off-spec should not be published at all.

SERIOUS-3 — The Oaktree finance-cost offsets are the same event counted twice

The memo nets incremental financing to CAD 15M/yr and then offsets it with (i) "retiring an instrument that … was not zero" carry and (ii) "consolidating 100% of Aecon Utilities' earnings to common holders." These are one event. Eliminating Oaktree's preferred is how 100% of Utilities' earnings accrue to common. Further: EBIT of CAD 214M is a consolidated figure that already includes 100% of Aecon Utilities' operating earnings; the preferred's claim sat below the operating line. The bull-ward double-credit lands directly in the CAD 60M finance-cost line — which is the line the "exactly CAD 2.12" claim depends on to within CAD 1M (see SERIOUS-5).

SERIOUS-4 — "Consensus is the clean-year bull case, achievable, not fantasy" is validated by fitted parameters, not evidence

Treated in full under SERIOUS-5. The generosity is real; the basis for it is not.

THE "EXACTLY CAD 2.12" CLAIM — VERIFIED, THEN DISMANTLED

SERIOUS-5 — The arithmetic is right; the "exactness" is manufactured by four chosen inputs

Recompute independently:

central : (0.034 × 6,288.6 − 60) × 0.735 ÷ 68.53 = (213.8 − 60) × 0.735 ÷ 68.53 = CAD 1.652 ✓ (1.65)
3.0%    : (188.66 − 60) × 0.735 ÷ 68.53                                        = CAD 1.380 ✓ (1.38)
4.1%    : (257.83 − 60) × 0.735 ÷ 68.53                                        = CAD 2.1218 ✓ (2.12)

The arithmetic is clean. Share count 68.53M is the correct post-issuance figure. Tax at 26.5% is the correct Ontario combined statutory rate. Finance cost 60 is consistent with the ~46M underlying run-rate (Q2 2026 finance cost 139.4M less the ~128M charge = ~11.4M/quarter) plus ~15M incremental. No arithmetic error. Credit given.

But the memo says: "That last number is not a coincidence — consensus forward EPS of CAD 2.12 is precisely a 4.1% consolidated operating margin." It is presented as external validation of the model's upper bound. Test the precision:

input varied consensus 2.12 implies a margin of
revenue 6,288.6 (memo, H2 at +11%) 4.10%
revenue 6,628.9 (H2 at H1's actual +22.1%) 3.89%
revenue 5,959 (TTM, flat) 4.32%
finance cost 46M instead of 60M 3.87%
finance cost 70M instead of 60M 4.26%
tax 25% instead of 26.5% 4.02%

The match to 4.1% is accurate to two decimal places only because four inputs were set where they were set. The honest statement is that consensus EPS of 2.12 implies an FY2026 consolidated operating margin somewhere in 3.87%–4.32% — a ±0.22-point band that is 40% as wide as the memo's entire 3.0–4.1% range.

And the deeper problem: the 4.1% ceiling and the consensus-implied margin share the revenue base (6,288.6) and are otherwise each a function of parameters the memo chose. Two quantities computed from overlapping chosen parameters landing 0.04 points apart is not a discovery. Move F from 185 to 0 and the memo's full-step endpoint is 4.59% against a consensus-implied 4.10% — no match at all. The "not a coincidence" is precisely a coincidence of parameter selection, and the memo builds "consensus is achievable, not fantasy" on top of it.

Not FATAL standing alone — it is not algebraically circular, and it happens to concede a bull point. It is FATAL as a component of FATAL-A, because the fitted 4.1% is what caps the bull ceiling at spot.

THE REMAINING SERIOUS FINDINGS

SERIOUS-6 — "Cycle amplitude is documented (−1.4% to +4.1% consolidated operating margin)" — the upper bound is modeled, not documented

This is the load-bearing support for rejecting Buffett's TOO UNCERTAIN challenge. The −1.4% is real (FY2024 operating loss 60.1M / 4.2B = −1.43%). The +4.1% has never been printed on any basis in this record. The documented consolidated operating margins are: FY2024 −1.43%, FY2025 +1.60% (87.1/5,430), H2 2025 +3.33%, H1 2026 +2.44%. FY2023 operating profit was never retrieved — the 3.5% in the FINANCIAL PHYSICS table is a net margin, confirmed against FY2025 (0.02B/5.43B = 0.37% ≈ 0.3%) and TTM (−0.11B/5.96B = −1.85% ≈ −2.0%).

So the memo bounds its uncertainty with one observed value at the bad end and one forecast value at the good end, calls the pair "documented," and uses the resulting narrowness to reject TOO HARD. The rejection is an assertion dressed as an observation.

This also reveals an over-concession. The memo struck its thesis sentence — "the market is pricing a margin Aecon has never printed" — entirely, on the strength of H2 2025's 3.33%. But the market is pricing 4.1%, and 4.1% has not been printed on a full-year basis, a half-year basis, or a quarterly basis anywhere in this file. The correct move was to narrow the sentence ("never printed for a full year, though printed 3.33% for a half"), not withdraw it. The memo threw away a true and load-bearing claim.

SERIOUS-7 — Load-Bearing Factor #2 carries a stale figure, overstated ~73%

Factor #2 is "Composition of the CAD 224M non-earnings cash-flow residual." The 224M was constructed in draft 1 as 368 − 18 − 106 − 20. The memo has since replaced its earnings base: it now uses normalized net income of CAD 113M ("CAD 304M against normalized net income of CAD 113M is FCF at 2.7x earnings"; 304/113 = 2.69 ✓; 1.65 × 68.53 = 113.1 ✓). Redo the residual on the memo's own current number:

368 (OCF) − 113 (normalized NI) − 106 (D&amp;A) − 20 (SBC) = CAD 129M

The unexplained residual is CAD 129M, not CAD 224M. The memo replaced every input that produced 224 and kept the output, then made it Load-Bearing Factor #2 and a named resolving disclosure.

SERIOUS-8 — The reinstated −25% volume breakeven contradicts the memo's own new fixed-cost model

Pass 1's SERIOUS-14 was correctly conceded and the −25% reconstruction reinstated. But the memo now has a second fixed-cost model in the same document. Run it: FY2026 contribution = 8.85%×2,885 + 9.94%×3,403.6 = CAD 593.6M against fixed cost of CAD 370M/yr. Blended contribution rate 9.44%. Operating breakeven revenue = 370/0.0944 = CAD 3,920M — a −38% decline, not −25%.

Two models of the same quantity, 13 points apart, both published, neither reconciled. The memo reinstated the less bullish of the two without noticing it had built a third. (And note the deeper issue: assuming CAD 370M/yr is genuinely fixed in a business where most cost is subcontract and materials overstates operating leverage — which is exactly what compresses the seasonal step in FATAL-A.)

SERIOUS-9 — The insider section still sizes one column

Pass 1's SERIOUS-6 was substantially fixed: both directions reported, both Lyash figures printed, neither resolved — compliant with the standing instruction. Credit.

But the sizing is still asymmetric on the memo's own chosen basis. It computes insider holdings of ~487,000 shares (0.71% × 68.53M) and correctly notes Rosenfeld's 125,000 = 25.7% of all insider stock. It does not run the same calculation on the buy side. On the larger of the two conflicting Lyash figures, 41,796 shares = 8.6% of the entire insider register — the second-largest insider transaction in the record and materially more than the CAD 138k the memo characterises. The memo dismisses the larger figure's price (US$47 ≈ CAD 65, impossible) but keeps using the smaller figure's share count without saying that it has done so. If the feeds disagree on both fields, you cannot take price from one and size from the other.

SERIOUS-10 — A double margin haircut, denied in the same sentence

The memo takes central 3.55% down to 3.4% "for project-loss recurrence," while declaring "I explicitly do not haircut further, because the 11–13x multiple below already carries the cyclicality." But the 3.0–4.1% range was built from H1 2025 (legacy losses live), H2 2025 (legacy cleanup) and H1 2026. Project losses are already in the base. Haircutting again for project-loss recurrence is the second penalty the memo says it isn't applying. Worth CAD 1.65/share at 12x — small on its own, bear-ward, and it compounds with FATAL-A and the +11% growth assumption.

SERIOUS-11 — The receivables range retains the endpoint pass 1 refuted

Re-derived correctly: 2025 AR 960M against norms of 13.6%/12.9%/11.9% × 5,430 gives excess of 221.5 / 259.5 / 313.8. The memo publishes 216–314, central 265. The 314 is the 2024 trough-base estimate that pass 1 struck as artifact-inflated — kept as the range top, and the "central 265" is the midpoint of the endpoints including it. Pass 1's defensible range was 220–262 (midpoint 241). The memo conceded the finding and then re-imported the refuted number, pulling the central up ~10% (CAD 0.35/share).

Mitigating, and this stands from pass 1: it is never deducted from value. Presentational, not valuation-bearing.

(b) HAS THE REVISION OVERCORRECTED? — NO. IT CONCEDED RHETORIC AND KEPT EVERY BEAR-WARD PARAMETER.

This is the most important section of this pass, and the answer runs opposite to what the shape of the revision suggests.

The memo moved bull/bear from 35/65 to 45/55, raised its band CAD 5, withdrew "no moat ever existed," withdrew the 50% haircut, withdrew the supplier-float story, withdrew "re-rating not earnings," withdrew the CAD 3/share plug, corrected the vote, reported both insider columns. Read as prose, it looks like a memo that gave a lot of ground.

Read as arithmetic, every undefended input still sits at the bear end. Inventory of the free parameters in Revision 2:

# parameter value chosen sourced? direction
1 fixed overhead F 185M/half no bear (caps bull 4.59→4.14%)
2 H2 2026 revenue growth +11% (H1 actual: +22.1%) no bear (~CAD 2/sh)
3 EV/EBIT multiple 11–13x (spot: 16.6x EV/EBITDA) no bear (CAD 9–19/sh)
4 margin haircut 3.55 → 3.4% asserted bear (CAD 1.65/sh)
5 net debt basis midpoint 334 (SEC: 174) no — false reconciliation bear (CAD 2.33/sh)
6 multiple applied to bull scenario cycle-discounted self-contradicting bear
7 tax on consolidated EBIT 26.5% incl. equity-accounted JV income (already taxed at JV level) technical bear
8 receivables central 265 (retains refuted 314) no bear (presentational)
9 FCF ceiling hurdle 9% (mandated 10–12%) no bull — and then unused

Eight bear-ward undefended choices; one bull-ward, which the memo then declined to act on. The concessions were made in the prose; the valuation was not moved by them.

Now price the correction. Take the memo's own central margin of 3.55% (before its undefended 3.4% haircut), the SEC debt basis of 174 (correct if the CAD 320.0M gap is the Oaktree preferred), H2 revenue at H1's actual growth, and the memo's own 13x ceiling:

FY2026 revenue      6,628.9
EBIT @ 3.55%          235.3
× 13x                3,058.9
− net debt 174       2,884.9
÷ 68.53M          CAD 42.09

The memo's own central case, with nothing changed but the unsourced parameters and using the memo's own multiple, reaches CAD 42.09 against a spot of CAD 43.55. The WAIT survives by 3%, not by 20%.

And the bull case, which the memo says "arrives at today's price," reaches CAD 56.34 at 13x on the gate's own full seasonal step — within CAD 0.66 of the analyst mean target of CAD 57.00 that the memo explicitly left "untested on the record" after withdrawing SERIOUS-10. The memo had the arithmetic to reach the consensus target on its own framework and never ran it. It withdrew the dismissal of the CAD 57 and never noticed it could derive the CAD 57.

The bull argument the memo is understating, stated plainly:

The memo's bull is "four quarters of 5% Adjusted EBITDA and normalized receivables." That is not the strongest bull available in this file. The strongest bull is the one the memo half-built and then capped:

  • H1 2026 Adjusted EBITDA CAD 131.7M vs CAD 38.6M — a +241% swing in the weak half [SEC].
  • H1 2026 operating profit +70.5M vs −15.0M — a CAD 85.5M swing in the weak half [SEC].
  • On the gate's own full seasonal step, FY2026 consolidated operating margin 4.59–4.68%.
  • A non-executive director bought at spot on 2026-07-31, the most recent insider transaction in the record — and on one of the two conflicting feeds, at 8.6% of the entire insider register.
  • Ontario has committed CAD 37B of capital for 2026–27 [SEARCH], and Darlington site prep came in on time and on budget [SEARCH].
  • Analyst mean target CAD 57.00, which the memo has left standing and which its own framework reaches at 13x.
  • A sovereign veto that legally excludes the highest-bidding class of acquirer from the CNSC-licensed constructor set — which the memo now correctly books in both directions but still does not let raise the multiple.

That case does not make Aecon a buy at CAD 43.55. It makes the honest statement "roughly fairly valued, with the resolution dated" — which the memo itself concedes in one line and then contradicts in its trigger: "If FY2026 lands at 4%+, fair value is CAD 37–44 and the stock is fairly priced today." A memo that says the stock may be fairly priced today should not publish a trigger of CAD 26–36 with 0% position at spot; those are different verdicts. The verdict and the trigger have come apart.

MINOR (b)

  • M-1: Taxing consolidated EBIT at 26.5% double-taxes equity-accounted JV earnings (Crosslinx, Mosaic, SA Energy), which are presented net of tax. Bear-ward, small, unremarked.
  • M-2: "Buy anywhere at or below CAD 36" is EV-negative on the memo's own 45/55 weights at 11–12x (weighted FV CAD 33.61); it is coherent only at the very top of its own multiple range (13x → weighted FV CAD 36.83). The trigger works only where the memo's own multiple assumption is most generous.
  • M-3: The 45/55 weights are asserted and never derived. One of the two stated reasons for the shift (backlog demotion) is invalid per SERIOUS-1.

THE REAL CHARLIE MUNGER'S TAKE

You did the hard thing. You took a public beating, you conceded seventeen findings without whining, and you rewrote the whole memo instead of patching it. That's rarer than a good idea and I'll say so before I take the hide off.

And you did something better than that. You went and built the margin instead of assuming it — pulled it out of the actual halves this company earns in — and then you had the honesty to publish that your build agreed with the fellow who beat you. I checked that. It's real. Move your overhead assumption anywhere from zero to double and your middle case stays at three and a half. That's a genuine second witness and it's the best work in the document.

Now. Where did you put your thumb?

You wrote down a hundred and eighty-five million dollars a half of fixed overhead. Where'd you get it? MG&A for the whole year was two hundred thirty-four. Your own FY2025 numbers say a hundred fifty-three. The only gross-profit number in the file is the one the dossier told you not to trust because it's the wrong segment. So you made it up. Fine — everybody makes something up. But look what it does. It doesn't touch your middle case at all. What it does is take the bull's ceiling from four-point-six down to four-point-one. And four-point-one is exactly the number that lets you write "the bull case arrives at today's price."

Then you ran a sensitivity — no step, half step, full step — on the one input that can't move your answer, while the input that does move it sat there frozen at a number nobody can source. Charlie, you know that trick. You caught it in someone else three weeks ago and got a medal for it.

Then the growth rate. This company grew twenty-two percent in the half you just finished. You put down eleven for the next one. Why? You can't say backlog — you'd just spent a page explaining that backlog is unusable. You can't demote the thermometer and then quote the temperature.

Then the multiple. Eleven to thirteen times operating profit. On a business trading at sixteen and a half times EBITDA, that's cutting the multiple in half, and your entire evidence for it is a sentence saying "contractors change hands in that band." There is no such band in this file. The dossier told you there is no peer set — the machine tried and handed you the same company twice. You obeyed the letter and broke the substance. And then you put out a ranked list of what moves the answer and left the multiple off it. Son, the multiple moves it further than the margin does. Ten to sixteen times is eighteen dollars a share. Your whole margin range is twelve.

And the debt. You said the red team's band and yours are "the same calculation on different debt bases." They aren't — his came off fifteen times a different margin. You'd said, four paragraphs earlier, that two mechanisms landing near each other is coincidence and not corroboration, and that it's a fatal standard. Then you used the coincidence. Also: that gap is three hundred twenty million on the nose, and the Oaktree preferred was marked to three hundred twenty million on the nose in the very quarter you're reading. You never once wondered. You took a midpoint, which is a number that's correct under no accounting treatment anybody uses, and you charged the same obligation once out of the cash and half again out of the debt.

Where I end up: you conceded in the paragraphs and kept every dollar. Eight loose parameters, eight of them set at the bear end, one at the bull end and you didn't even spend that one. Loosen only the ones you never sourced — keep your own margin, your own multiple — and your central case comes to forty-two against a price of forty-three fifty. That's not a mispricing. That's a rounding error with a footnote.

You said the fully-proven bull only gets you to today's price. Run it with the seasonal step you were handed and the debt figure that's on the balance sheet and it gets you to fifty-six — which is, and I want you to sit with this, sixty-six cents from the analyst target you just admitted you had no business dismissing. You withdrew the attack on the fifty-seven and never noticed your own arithmetic produces it.

Sit on your ass. But say the true thing while you're sitting: it's about fairly priced and you'd rather own something else.

THE REAL WARREN BUFFETT'S TAKE

I raised the "too hard" objection last time and this fellow answered me — properly, in writing, and he didn't hide behind me. That deserves a serious reply.

He says the uncertainty is bounded and dated rather than open, and he bounds it minus-one-point-four to plus-four-point-one. Well now. Where did the four-point-one come from? Minus one-four is a thing that happened — 2024, it's in the filings. Four-point-one is a thing he forecast, this week, on his own model. You can't bound a range with one fact and one forecast and call the pair "documented." The best operating margin this company has actually printed anywhere in this file is three-and-a-third, for one half, and one-point-six for a full year.

Which brings me to something he shouldn't have given away. His first draft said the market is pricing a margin Aecon has never printed, and he struck the whole sentence because the back half of 2025 came in at three-point-three. But the market isn't pricing three-point-three. It's pricing four-point-one, and four-point-one has never been printed here on any basis at all. He should have narrowed that sentence, not withdrawn it. When a man beats you fairly on a point, give him the point — don't give him the two next to it as well.

On the director who bought at today's price on the thirty-first of July. He reports it now, and he reports both of the conflicting sizes without pretending to settle them, and I asked for exactly that. But he sized the seller — a quarter of all the insider stock, he says, and that's right — and didn't size the buyer. On the bigger of the two numbers that purchase is eight and a half percent of every share every insider owns. Maybe that figure is wrong. But you don't get to take the price from one report and the share count from the other and not tell anybody.

Now, Rule Number One. What actually protects a man here? Six hundred thirty million of cash, a year and three-quarters of backlog, and a quarter each of two toll bridges he doesn't control. Three hundred twenty of that cash is spoken for — he's right and he's fixed the arithmetic he got wrong. But the three hundred twenty he keeps writing down turns up again in the debt figure he chose, and eight-oh-four minus four-eight-four is three-twenty exactly. Somebody ought to have picked up a telephone about that before splitting a difference.

Is it a toll bridge? Still no. Ninety-nine point nine percent of the revenue gets re-bid every procurement. But I'll tell you what changed my mind a little since the last draft: the government of Canada legally stopped a foreign buyer from owning this thing on national-security grounds. He counts that both ways now, which is right. He just won't let it touch the multiple — and a fence a competitor cannot buy his way through is the only kind of fence there is.

Would Berkshire buy it? Still no, and still for the shape rather than the price. But I'll withdraw half of my objection: he did bound it, more honestly than most, and the resolving events do have dates on them. What I won't accept is a memo that computes fair value at forty-two on its own central case, computes the good outcome at fifty-something, tells the reader "if it's four percent the stock is fairly priced today" — and then prints a trigger of twenty-six to thirty-six and calls the position zero. The trigger and the verdict have come apart, and the trigger is the part the reader will act on.

If you think it's fairly valued, say fairly valued. That's an honest answer and there's no shame in it.

THE "OLD SCHOOL" FINAL SCORECARD

  • Circle of Competence: In (understandable) / marginal (forecastable). The memo's bounding argument is a genuine improvement and I upgrade it from Pass 1 — the amplitude is real, the events are dated. But the upper bound of that amplitude is a forecast wearing an observation's clothes.
  • Moat Integrity: Narrow, cyclical, unproven above cost of capital. The revision reached the right verdict here and counts the CCCI national-security block in both directions, as it should. Remaining defect: it books the sovereign entry barrier as moat evidence and then refuses to let it touch the 11–13x multiple, where moat evidence is supposed to show up.
  • Management Character: Mixed, leaning Promoter. Sherlock's sequence survives a second pass intact and remains the strongest independent finding in the council. The insider section is now two-sided — genuinely fixed — but still sized on one side only.
  • Berkshire Buy? No. Unchanged, and for shape rather than price.
  • Graham-Dodd Grade: C− for the security (negative Graham Floor, 3.1x book, 16.6x EV/EBITDA, 20.5x forward on an unproven margin, 1.76% carry). B/B+ for the memo — up from B−. Seventeen honest concessions, a genuinely independent margin rebuild, exemplary VIC and superinvestor discipline. Held below A− by four fatal defects all located inside the replacement reasoning, and by a pattern in which every parameter the memo did not source sits at the bear end.

VERDICT ON THE VERDICT

WAIT is no longer supported by the memo's own arithmetic. "Roughly fairly valued, resolution dated Q3/Q4 2026" is.

The memo's central case, with only the unsourced parameters neutralised and its own margin and its own multiple retained, is CAD 42.09 against a spot of CAD 43.55. Its bull case, on the gate's own seasonal step, is CAD 49–56 — not "today's price." The memo has proven the stock is approximately fairly valued and published a trigger 17–40% below it.

The band CAD 26–36 does not stand. On the memo's own numbers, corrected only where it failed to source:

  • CAD 38 is the defensible ceiling (3.4% at 13x on the SEC debt basis — which is the memo's own top row, before the fabricated midpoint).
  • CAD 42 is the ceiling on the memo's own central margin of 3.55% with H2 revenue growing at H1's actual rate.
  • CAD 26 at the floor is unchanged and remains sound as a back-up-the-truck level.

The gap between "wait for a 15% pullback" and "wait for a 20% pullback" is not what matters here. What matters is that a memo which concedes "if FY2026 lands at 4%+ … the stock is fairly priced today" and then computes 4%+ as its own vindicated bull case has written a fairly-valued conclusion and printed an expensive verdict.

PART 1 — THE SEVEN ADJUDICATIONS

1. THE F = 184.7 REBUTTAL — HALF CONCEDED, HALF FATAL

1a. The Q1 print exists. Pass 2 was wrong. Conceded without reservation.

I opened the raw file. It is there, and the memo's citation is accurate:

/tmp/silicon_council/ARE.TO/initial_dossier.md
line 258:  SOURCE: Aecon reports first quarter 2026 results with record backlog ...
line 264:  |  | $ millions (except per share amounts) |  |  | March 31 | | | |
line 265:  |  |  |  |  | 2026 |  |  | 2025 |
line 267:  |  | Revenue |  | $ | 1,257.0 |  | $ | 1,061.7 |
line 268:  |  | Gross profit |  |  | 100.5 |

Three tests, all passed: - It exists. Pass 2 wrote "H1 2026 consolidated gross profit is not in the record." That sentence was false. I retrieved the Q2 print and stopped searching. My error, stated plainly. - It is consolidated. Revenue of 1,257.0 is the group total — Construction Q1 revenue was CAD 1,254M and Concessions CAD 2M [refined_dossier lines 143–144], summing to the same figure. The prior-year column 1,061.7 is likewise the consolidated comparative. This is the press-release summary table, not a segment note. - It is on the basis claimed. Three months ended March 31, CAD millions, gross profit line.

The arithmetic sums as stated: 100.5 + 154.7 = 255.2; 255.2 − 70.5 = 184.7. F = 184.7 is arithmetically correct and half of it is unambiguously filed. Pass 2's charge that the number "was made up" does not stand and I withdraw it.

1b. FATAL-E — But the memo's answer to the segment warning answers a question nobody asked, and its own components falsify "fixed."

The non-sequitur first. The memo writes: "the segment worry does not bite at the gross-profit line: Concessions revenue was CAD 2M in each quarter — 0.1% of the total — so segment and consolidated gross profit differ by roughly a rounding error."

That is not what the dossier warned about. Read the warning [refined_dossier line 135]:

> "A second set of Q2 2026 figures appears in the record — gross profit CAD 154.7M (9.5%), Adjusted EBITDA CAD 89.7M (5.5%), operating profit CAD 58.5M (3.6%) — while the press release headline gives consolidated operating profit of CAD 36.4M and Adjusted EBITDA of CAD 82.4M. The CAD 58.5M figure is explicitly identified elsewhere in the same release as Construction segment operating profit... Segment total ≈ CAD 59.7M against consolidated CAD 36.4M implies roughly CAD 23M of corporate cost / eliminations not attributed to either segment."

The gap between that block and the consolidated numbers is corporate cost and eliminations, not Concessions revenue. The memo rebuts the Concessions point (which the dossier never made, and which is trivially true) and leaves the corporate/eliminations point (which the dossier did make, and which is the whole warning) untouched. And eliminations land in cost of revenue: the dossier names "deferral of intercompany construction profit on network assets under development" as an item in this very quarter [line 144]. That is a gross-profit-line item.

Now the falsification, which pass 2 could not run because it did not have the Q1 print. Decompose F by quarter using only figures the memo itself uses:

Q1 2026 consolidated operating profit = H1 70.5 − Q2 36.4        = 34.1
Q1 overhead  = gross profit 100.5 − operating profit 34.1        = 66.4
Q2 overhead  = gross profit 154.7 − operating profit 36.4        = 118.3
                                                          sum    = 184.7 ✓

Fixed overhead of CAD 66.4M in Q1 and CAD 118.3M in Q2 — a 78% jump between consecutive quarters. That is not a fixed cost. It is either (i) a basis mismatch, exactly as the dossier warned, or (ii) proof that the quantity the model holds constant is not constant.

Either branch kills the method. Run it the other way for the sharpest form: the memo's own model, applied at quarterly granularity, contradicts the very print it cites as its sourcing. If F really is fixed at 92.35/quarter, then Q1 gross profit should have been 34.1 + 92.35 = 126.5. The filed figure is 100.5. The memo cites line 268 to prove F is filed, and line 268 refutes the model that uses F.

And the corroborating decomposition double-counts. The memo writes: "F decomposes almost exactly as the dossier implies: MG&A at 4.1% of H1 revenue ≈ 118, corporate/eliminations ≈ 46/half, residual ≈ 20 for acquired-intangible amortisation." (118 + 46 + 20 = 184.)

  • The 4.1% MG&A ratio is the consolidated MG&A line [refined_dossier line 249: "MG&A fell to 4.1% of revenue from 4.5%"]. Consolidated MG&A already contains corporate MG&A. Adding "corporate/eliminations ≈ 46" on top of it counts the same cost twice.
  • Test the structure against FY2025, where every input is filed: GP 394.1 − OP 87.1 = F 307.0, of which MG&amp;A 234.0 (the 4.1%) and other 73.0. Scale that structure to H1 2026: MG&amp;A 4.1% × 2,888 = 118.4 plus other ≈ 36.5 → F ≈ 155, not 185.
  • The third term is defined as "residual ≈ 20," i.e. whatever is left. Three terms, one of which is a plug, summing to the target by construction. That is not a decomposition; it is a remainder dressed as corroboration. CHECK 0 violation.

FATAL-E, scoped precisely. The sentence "F = 184.7 is a filed number, not an assumption, and it resolves the reconciliation gap rather than papering over it" must not publish. The sum is filed-ish; the fixed premise the model rests on is refuted by its own components; the reconciliation gap is papered over, not resolved, against an explicit dossier instruction not to; and the supporting decomposition is fitted.

Magnitude, stated honestly so this is not overcharged. Test the alternative — hold Q1's implied CAD 66.4M overhead constant, giving consolidated Q2 gross profit of ~102.8, H1 gross margin 7.04% and F ≈ 132.8/half. Roll forward on the memo's own method: the bull ceiling falls from 4.67% to ~4.44%, the central from ~3.65% to ~3.49%. Roughly 0.15–0.25 margin points, CAD 1–2/share. The qualitative shape of the memo's margin table survives. The defect is in the claim, not in the answer — but the claim is the headline of the section.

Note the sign has flipped. In Revision 2, F was bear-ward (it capped the bull ceiling). In Revision 3 it is bull-ward: a higher F combined with a higher gross margin produces more operating leverage at rising revenue, and the memo's bull EBIT of CAD 310M and bull ceiling of 4.67% both depend on it. The memo does not say so.
